QUESTION: After I create an inspection record in Audit History, what do the Save, Upload and Next buttons do, and which fields must I complete?
JUDGING RULE: SCORE_BY: Must state: all mandatory fields marked with an asterisk are completed; Save stores the inspection details; Upload attaches the required supporting documents; Next moves on to the Observation page (the destination matters, not the word 'next'). | must ['mandatory', 'save', 'upload']; must_not ['not covered', 'not documented', 'does not describe']; expected_module None

* Click **+ New Record** to add an inspection record. (See **Figure 4.**)

screenshot: SIRE 2.0 History page showing the + New Record button

Figure 4

* <u>Select the **Vessel** from the dropdown menu.</u>

* <u>Select the desired **Inspection Type** from the dropdown menu.</u>

* Click **Add New** to create the inspection record. (See **Figure 5.**)

screenshot: Manual Data Entry modal with Vessel and Inspection Type dropdowns and Add New button

Figure 5

---

[5] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Document (p.13))
* <u>The inspection and observation details are automatically imported, and an inspection record</u> <u>is created.</u>

* Click **Upload** to add supporting attachments manually, if required. (See **Figure 15.**)

screenshot_from_computer: SIRE 2.0 interface showing Inspection Details form with callouts: "The system automatically imports the Inspection and Observation details and creates the corresponding inspection record." and "Click on 'Upload' to add the attachments manually."

Figure 15

[icon: pointing hand] Observation details, including **Positive** and **LAE** findings, are automatically populated from the uploaded JSON file.

[icon: pointing hand] Additional supporting attachments, if any, must be uploaded manually.

FULL ANSWER:
Audit History supports two ways to create an inspection record:

1. **Manual data entry**
   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select the **Vessel**.
   3. Select the **Inspection Type**.
   4. Click **Add New**.
   5. Complete all inspection-detail fields marked with **\***.
   6. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
   7. Click **Upload** to attach supporting documents or attachments.
   8. Click **Next** to proceed to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

2. **SIRE/CDI file upload**
   1. Click **New Record**.
   2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
   3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
   4. Click **Upload**. SAIL automatically creates the inspection record and imports the inspection and observation details.
   5. Click **Upload** again if additional supporting attachments must be added manually.

**Source:**  
- Manual data entry — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §§2.1 Method 1 and 2.1.1 Inspection Record Page, pp.7–8.  
- SIRE/CDI upload — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.2 Method 2, p.11; *Document*, p.13.

screenshot_from_computer: SIRE 2.0 interface showing Inspection Details form with callouts: "The system automatically imports the Inspection and Observation details and creates the corresponding inspection record." and "Click on 'Upload' to add the attachments manually."

[icon: pointing hand] Observation details, including **Positive** and **LAE** findings, are automatically populated from the uploaded JSON file.

[icon: pointing hand] Additional supporting attachments, if any, must be uploaded manually.

FULL ANSWER:
There are two supported ways to create an inspection record:

1. **Manual data entry**
   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select the **Vessel**.
   3. Select the **Inspection Type**.
   4. Click **Add New**.
   5. Complete all inspection-record fields marked with **\***.
   6. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
   7. Click **Upload** to attach supporting documents or attachments. Attachments are displayed under the Upload icon.
   8. Click **Next** to go to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

2. **SIRE/CDI file upload**
   1. Click **New Record**.
   2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
   3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
   4. Click **Upload**. The inspection record is created automatically, with inspection and observation details imported from the file.
   5. Click **Upload** again only if additional supporting attachments must be added manually.

The excerpts do not name the individual mandatory fields; they specify that every field marked **\*** must be completed.

**Source:**  
- Manual data entry — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.1 Method 1 (p.7) and §2.1.1 Inspection Record Page (p.8).  
- SIRE/CDI upload — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.2 Method 2 (p.11) and Document (p.13).

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support two ways to create an inspection record:

1. **Manual data entry**
   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select the **Vessel**.
   3. Select the **Inspection Type**.
   4. Click **Add New**.
   5. On the inspection details page, complete all mandatory fields marked with **\***.
   6. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
   7. Click **Upload** to attach required supporting documents; attachments are displayed under the Upload icon.
   8. Click **Next** to proceed to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

2. **SIRE/CDI file upload**
   1. Click **New Record**.
   2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
   3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
   4. Click **Upload**. The inspection record is created automatically, with inspection and observation details imported.
   5. Use **Upload** again to add supporting attachments manually, if required. The excerpts do not state that **Save** or **Next** is used in this upload process.

**Source:**  
- Manual data entry: *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §§2.1 Method 1 and 2.1.1 Inspection Record Page, pp.7–8.  
- SIRE/CDI upload: *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.2 Method 2, p.11; *Document*, p.13.

FULL ANSWER:
After creating an inspection record:

1. Complete all mandatory fields marked with an asterisk (**\***).
2. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
3. Click **Upload** to attach required supporting documents. Attachments related to the inspection are displayed under the Upload icon.
4. Click **Next** to proceed to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

For a record created through **SIRE/CDI file upload**, the inspection and observation details are imported automatically and the record is created. Use **Upload** to add any additional supporting attachments manually, if required.

**Source:**  
- Manual data entry — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §§2.1 and 2.1.1, pp.7–8.  
- SIRE/CDI upload — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.2, p.11; Document, p.13.

FULL ANSWER:
Two supported ways to create an inspection record are:

1. **Manual data entry**
   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select the **Vessel** and **Inspection Type**, then click **Add New**.
   3. Complete all mandatory fields marked with **\***.
   4. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
   5. Click **Upload** to attach required supporting documents. Attachments are displayed under the Upload icon.
   6. Click **Next** to proceed to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

2. **SIRE/CDI file upload**
   1. Click **New Record**.
   2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
   3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
   4. Click **Upload**. The inspection record is created automatically, with inspection and observation details imported.
   5. Click **Upload** again to add supporting attachments manually, if required. The uploaded JSON may automatically populate Positive and LAE findings.

The excerpts do not list the individual mandatory fields; they only specify that every field marked with **\*** must be completed.

**Source:**  
- Manual data entry — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.1 Method 1, p.7; §2.1.1 Inspection Record Page, p.8.  
- SIRE/CDI upload — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.2 Method 2, p.11; Document, p.13.

FULL ANSWER:
After creating an inspection record, the supported routes are:

**Manual data entry**

1. Click **+ New Record**.
2. Select the **Vessel**.
3. Select the **Inspection Type**.
4. Click **Add New**.
5. On the inspection record page, complete all mandatory fields marked with **\***.
6. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
7. Click **Upload** to attach supporting documents. Related attachments are displayed under the **Upload** icon.
8. Click **Next** to proceed to the **Observation** page and enter observation details, as applicable.

**SIRE/CDI file upload**

1. Click **New Record**.
2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
4. Click **Upload**. SAIL automatically creates the inspection record and imports the inspection and observation details.
5. If required, click **Upload** on the record to add supporting attachments manually.

For the file-upload route, the excerpts do not state that **Save** or **Next** must be used, nor do they specify additional fields to complete; the imported inspection and observation details are populated automatically.

**Source:** Manual data entry — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.1 Method 1 and §2.1.1, pp.7–8; SIRE/CDI upload — same manual, §2.2 Method 2, p.11, and *Document*, p.13.

FULL ANSWER:
After creating an inspection record, the supported routes are:

**Manual data entry** — select the Vessel and Inspection Type, then click **Add New**. On the inspection details page:

1. Complete **all mandatory fields marked with an asterisk (*)**.
2. Click **Save** to save the inspection details.
3. Click **Upload** to attach the required supporting documents or attachments. Attachments are displayed under the Upload icon.
4. Click **Next** to proceed to the Observation page and enter observation details, as applicable.

**SIRE/CDI file upload** — select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, choose the file, and click **Upload**. The inspection and observation details are then imported automatically and the inspection record is created. Any additional supporting attachments can be uploaded manually if required. The excerpts do not specify additional fields that must be completed manually for this route.

- Manual data entry: *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.1 Method 1, p.7; §2.1.1 Inspection Record Page, p.8.

1. **Manual data entry**
   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select the **Vessel**.
   3. Select the **Inspection Type**.

   1. Click **+ New Record**.
   2. Select **SIRE 2.0 JSON**, **CDI XML**, or **SIRE XML**, as applicable.
   3. Click **Choose File** and select the required file.
   4. Click **Upload**. SAIL automatically creates the inspection record and imports the inspection and observation details.
   5. If needed, click **Upload** on the inspection details page to add supporting attachments manually.

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can filter the Office-shared fleet notifications in either of these ways:

1. Click **Filters**.
2. Select the required **Period**, or enter a notification **title**.
3. Click **Apply** to display matching records.
4. Click **Clear** to reset the filters and show the unfiltered list.

From a notification in the list, you can:

1. Use the **View** action to open the notification.
2. Review its title, description, and associated information. Section 1 is view-only for vessel users and cannot be edited.
3. Use **View** to access and download attachments shared by the Office.
4. The list also displays an **Edit** action icon, but the manual specifies that the notification details in Section 1 cannot be edited by vessel users.

**Source:**  
- Filtering and list actions: *Incident - Fleet Notification User Manual_Vessel R0_20.03.2026*, “2. Fleet Notification Overview,” pp. 5, 8.  
- Viewing details and attachments: same manual, “3. Fleet Notification Form,” p. 5.

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can filter the Office-shared fleet notifications in these ways:

**Filter by period**
1. Open the **Fleet Notification** screen.
2. Click **Filters**.
3. Select the required **Period**.
4. Click **Apply** to display matching records, or **Clear** to reset the filter.

**Search by title**
1. Click **Filters**.
2. Enter the notification title in the **title** field.
3. Click **Apply**, or click **Clear** to reset the search.

For a notification in the list:

1. Use its action icon to **view** the notification.
2. Review the title, description, and associated information. Section 1 is view-only for vessel users and cannot be edited.
3. Use **View** to access and download attachments shared by the Office.
4. The list also displays an edit action icon, but the manual states that vessel users cannot edit the notification information in Section 1.

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can filter notifications in two ways:

1. Click **Filters**.
2. Select the required **Period**, then click **Apply**. Use **Clear** to reset the filter.
3. Alternatively, enter a notification **title**, then click **Apply**. Use **Clear** to remove the search.

For a notification in the filtered list:

1. Use the **View** action icon to open it.
2. Review the title, description, and associated information. These details are view-only for vessel users and cannot be edited.
3. Use **View** to access and download attachments shared by the Office.
4. The list also shows an **Edit** action icon, but the manual only states that Section 1 details cannot be edited by vessel users.

Source: Fleet Notification User Manual_Vessel, “Fleet Notification Overview” (pp. 5, 8) — filtering by period/title and notification actions; “Fleet Notification Form” (p. 5) — viewing details and attachments.

FULL ANSWER:
For fleet notifications shared by the Office, a vessel user can:

1. **Filter the list**
   1. Click **Filters**.
   2. Select the required **Period**, or enter a notification **title**.
   3. Click **Apply** to display matching records.
   4. Click **Clear** to reset the filter.

2. **View a notification**
   1. Select the notification’s **View** action icon.
   2. Review its title, description, and associated information.
   3. Use **View** to access and download attachments shared by the Office.
   4. The information in **Section 1 – Description** is view-only and cannot be edited by vessel users.

3. **Use the edit action**
   1. Select the notification’s **Edit** action icon shown in the list.
   2. Section 1 description information remains non-editable for vessel users.

**Source:** Filtering and list actions — *Fleet Notification User Manual_Vessel*, “2. FLEET NOTIFICATION OVERVIEW,” pp. 5, 8. Viewing and attachment access — same manual, “3. FLEET NOTIFICATION FORM,” p. 5.

<u>Figure 3</u>

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can filter the Office-shared fleet notifications in either of these ways:

1. Click **Filters**.
2. Select the required **Period**, then click **Apply** to display matching notifications.
3. Alternatively, enter a notification **title**, then click **Apply**.
4. Click **Clear** to reset the filters.

For a notification in the list:

1. Use the **View** action icon to open and review its details.
2. In **Section 1 – Description**, you can review the title, description, and associated information, but vessel users cannot edit this information.
3. Use **View** to access and download attachments shared by the Office.
4. The overview also displays an **Edit** action icon for selected notifications; however, the manual specifically states that the description information is view-only for vessel users.

**Source:**  
- Period/title filtering and notification actions — *Incident - Fleet Notification User Manual_Vessel*, **Fleet Notification Overview**, pp. 5 and 8.  
- Viewing details and downloading attachments — *Incident - Fleet Notification User Manual_Vessel*, **Fleet Notification Form**, p. 5.

FULL ANSWER:
You can filter candidate records in these Crewing areas:

1. **In Progress**
   1. Click **Filter**.
   2. Use the search field and filters such as **Rank Applied, Vessel Type, Nationality, Status,** and **Manning Agent**.
   3. Click **Apply** to filter the results, or **Clear** to reset the selection.
   4. To filter an individual column, click the **filter icon in that column’s header**.

2. **Rejected** — the steps are the same as **In Progress**, section **1.2.1.2**, page 13:
   1. Click **Filter**.
   2. Search or select filters such as **Rank Applied, Vessel Type, Nationality, Status,** and **Manning Agent**.
   3. Click **Apply**, or **Clear** to reset.
   4. Use the filter icon in a column header for individual-column filtering.

3. **Recruited** — the steps are the same as **In Progress**, section **1.2.1.2**, page 13:
   1. Click **Filter**.
   2. Search or select filters such as **Rank Applied, Vessel Type, Nationality, Status,** and **Manning Agent**.
   3. Click **Apply**, or **Clear** to reset.
   4. Use the filter icon in a column header for individual-column filtering.

**Source:**
- In Progress: *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, section 1.2.1.2, p. 13.
- Rejected: *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, section 1.2.4.4, p. 23; resolved cross-reference to section 1.2.1.2, p. 13.
- Recruited: *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, section 1.2.2.4, p. 21; resolved cross-reference to section 1.2.1.2, p. 13.

FULL ANSWER:
To filter candidate records:

1. Click **Filter**.
2. Use the search field and filters such as **Rank Applied, Vessel Type, Nationality, Status**, and **Manning Agent**.
3. Click **Apply** to display the filtered results, or **Clear** to reset the selections.

For **Rejected** and **Recruited** candidates, the steps are the same as **In Progress**, section **1.2.1.2, page 13**.

To filter an individual column:

1. Click the **filter icon in the relevant column header**.
2. Apply the column-specific filter.
3. Click **Clear** to reset the column-level filter where available.

Source: In Progress — Crewing User Manual, §1.2.1.2, p.13; Rejected — §1.2.4.4, p.23; Recruited — §1.2.2.4, p.21.

FULL ANSWER:
You can filter candidate records in these ways:

1. **Use the overall candidate filters**
   1. Click **Filter**.
   2. Enter a search term and/or select filters such as **Rank Applied, Vessel Type, Nationality, Status**, and **Manning Agent**.

2. **Filter an individual column**
   1. Click the **filter icon** in the relevant table-column header.
   2. Set the column filter.
   3. Use **Clear** if you need to reset the filters.

For **Rejected** and **Recruited** candidates, the same steps apply as for **In Progress**, according to the resolved cross-references.

* Click the 'Select Vessels' dropdown and select one or more vessels to configure and view vessel-specific certificate records. (Ref Figure 19)

* Select the 'Applicable' checkbox to assign the certificate to the selected vessel; clear it to remove the assignment. (Ref Figure 19)

* Click the 'Save' button to update the records. (Ref Figure 19)

Screenshot of Ship Certificates Admin interface showing vessel selection and certificate assignment with callouts: 1. Click here to choose the Multi-select vessel dropdown. 2. Click here to assign or unassign the certificate. 3. Click here to update records.

Figure 19

FULL ANSWER:
In the Office **Certificates** screen:

1. Click **Filter**.
2. Select a **Vessel** to view vessel-specific certificate records.
3. Refine the records using:
   - **Fleet**
   - **Add Group**
   - **Due Status** — such as **All**, **Overdue**, or **Upcoming Due**
4. Click **Clear** to reset all applied filters.

Source: Certificates — *How to Apply Filter*, section **1.1.3.2**, page 7.

4. Click **Clear** to reset all applied filters.

* Click the 'Select Vessels' dropdown and select one or more vessels to configure and view vessel-specific certificate records. (Ref Figure 19)

* Select the 'Applicable' checkbox to assign the certificate to the selected vessel; clear it to remove the assignment. (Ref Figure 19)

* Click the 'Save' button to update the records. (Ref Figure 19)

Screenshot of Ship Certificates Admin interface showing vessel selection and certificate assignment with callouts: 1. Click here to choose the Multi-select vessel dropdown. 2. Click here to assign or unassign the certificate. 3. Click here to update records.

Figure 19

FULL ANSWER:
In the Office **Certificates** screen, you can narrow records using:

1. Click **Filter**.
2. Select a **Vessel** to view vessel-specific certificate records.
3. Use **Fleet**, **Add Group**, and **Due Status** filters. Due Status options include **All**, **Overdue**, and **Upcoming Due**.
4. Click **Clear** to reset all applied filters.

Source: [1] Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, §1.1.3.2 “How to Apply Filter,” p. 7.

[1] (Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026 — 4. HOW TO CREATE A NEW MOC (p.8))
* A new MoC can be created using any of the following methods: <u>(See Figure 6)</u>

1. Click **"+ New MoC"** on the **Dashboard** page.

2. Navigate to **MoC Log** and select **"+ New MoC"** on the top right corner.

3. Select **New MoC** from the menu to start creating a new record.

screenshot_from_computer: MOC Dashboard showing three options to create a new MoC

<u>Figure 6</u>

---

[2] (Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026 — 5. HOW TO FILL MOC FORM – PART A (p.9))
1. Select “Vessel” or “Office” based on the MoC being created.

2. Complete the required fields under **Part A**.

3. Add attachments using the **Upload** button. Attachments can be viewed under **View**.

4. Click “**Save**” to generate the **MoC No./ID** and continue later if required.

5. After completing and reviewing the information, click “**Submit**”.

FULL ANSWER:
From the Office MoC system, a new MoC can be started in three ways:

1. On the **Dashboard**, click **“+ New MoC”**.
2. Open **MoC Log**, then click **“+ New MoC”** in the top-right corner.
3. Open the menu and select **“New MoC”**.

**Source:**
- Dashboard option — *Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026*, **4. How To Create A New MoC**, p. 8.
- MoC Log option — same manual and section, p. 8.
- Menu option — same manual and section, p. 8.

<u>Figure 9</u>

FULL ANSWER:
From the Office system, you can start a new MoC in three ways:

1. Click **“+ New MoC”** on the **Dashboard** page.
2. Open **MoC Log**, then click **“+ New MoC”** in the top-right corner.
3. Select **“New MoC”** from the menu.

After starting the record, select **“Office”** in Part A when the MoC is office-related.

**Source:**  
- Methods 1–3: *Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026*, Section 4, “How To Create A New MoC,” p. 8.  
- Office selection: *Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026*, Section 5, “How To Fill MOC Form – Part A,” p. 9.

FRESH VALIDATION — CASE fresh-wo-broad-1
QUESTION: What are the different ways a work order can come into existence in PMS?
JUDGING RULE: SCORE_BY: Both manual routes are required: the Components screen 'Generate WO' for a selected component (choose a reason), and '+ Unplanned W.O' on the Work Orders screen. Attribution rule: automatic ship-side generation and the office 'Generate Now' action appear ONLY in the draft code-derived guidance, so if the answer mentions either it must say that this comes from draft guidance rather than the manuals. Presenting unplanned creation as the only way is a defect. | must ['generate wo', 'unplanned']; must_not ['only way', 'not covered']; expected_module None

==============================================================================================================
[B0] run 1 — automatic verdict: {'answer': True, 'module': True, 'citation': True}
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order
  [2] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28)
  [3] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23)
  [4] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.29)
  [5] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.32)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

---

[3] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23))
* Work Orders (WO) is a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.29))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 43)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 43)

* Click on the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button to create an unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 43)

* Enter all required job details in Part A and click Save to store it as a draft. Then complete Part B and click Submit Work Order to generate the unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 44)

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.32))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 49)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 49)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 49)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 49)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with four callout boxes: 1. Click on the 'Work Order'. 2. Click here to select a vessel. 3. Choose the required field from the status tab that needs to be completed. 4. The work order form can be opened by clicking the 'Edit' icon or selecting the table row.

Figure 49

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 50)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 50)

Screenshot of the Part B - Work Completion Record form with three callout boxes: 1. Click here to open the 'Part B' section and enter the required details. 2. From here, the user can upload the docs. 3. Use this section to add or select quick inputs and smart suggestions.

Figure 50

\* To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

FULL ANSWER:
Work orders can come into existence in three ways:

1. **Planned, scheduled generation**
   - **On Ship:** the ship system creates work orders automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action is required.
   - **In Office:** a **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is off by default.
   - The draft code-derived guidance says these planned-generation options exist. The June published manuals do not describe them.

2. **On-demand generation for one job**
   - Go to **Components**; in the Office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - There is no special role check, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.

3. **Unplanned work order**
   - Go to **Work Orders**; in the Office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to create the work order.
   - No special role check or generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

The draft code-derived guidance states the Office switch and Sail Admin conditions above. The June published manuals describe the on-demand and unplanned procedures but do not state those Office conditions.

**Source:**
- Planned generation — *Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “Planned (scheduled) work orders” (draft code-derived guidance, repository revision cf5241ad6).
- One-job on-demand generation — same guidance, “One job on demand” (draft code-derived guidance, repository revision cf5241ad6).
- Unplanned work order — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; and the draft guidance, “Unplanned work.”

FULL ANSWER:
A work order can come into existence in three ways:

1. **Planned or scheduled generation**
   - On the **ship**, the system creates work orders automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is required.
   - In the **office**, nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, provided that vessel’s **office work-order generation switch is ON**. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is off by default.
   - The job must not already have an active work order.

2. **On-demand generation for one job**
   - Go to **Components**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, click **Generate WO**, and select **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - There is no role check, although normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation switch must be ON**.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Go to **Work Orders**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**, complete **Part A**, and click **Save**.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to generate it.
   - There is no role check or generation switch requirement; normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

The first set of details comes from **draft, code-derived guidance** at repository revision **cf5241ad6**. The June published manuals describe the on-demand and unplanned procedures, but do not describe automatic ship generation or the office **Generate Now** function and do not state the office conditions.

**Source:**  
- Planned/scheduled generation: *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “Planned (scheduled) work orders” (draft code-derived guidance, revision cf5241ad6).  
- One-job on-demand generation: same guidance, “One job on demand”; June Office/Sail Admin manual, procedure **1.1.5.6** is related to completing—not creating—a work order.  
- Unplanned work: *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, **1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER**, p.29; also the draft three-ways guidance.

Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.32))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 49)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 49)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 49)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 49)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with four callout boxes: 1. Click on the 'Work Order'. 2. Click here to select a vessel. 3. Choose the required field from the status tab that needs to be completed. 4. The work order form can be opened by clicking the 'Edit' icon or selecting the table row.

Figure 49

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 50)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 50)

Screenshot of the Part B - Work Completion Record form with three callout boxes: 1. Click here to open the 'Part B' section and enter the required details. 2. From here, the user can upload the docs. 3. Use this section to add or select quick inputs and smart suggestions.

Figure 50

\* To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

FULL ANSWER:
A work order can come into existence in three ways:

1. **Planned, scheduled generation**
   - **On the ship:** the system creates it automatically once a day from the job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is required.
   - **In the office:** nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is off by default.
   - This automatic-generation and office-Generate-Now information comes from draft, code-derived guidance; the June manuals do not describe it.

2. **On-demand generation for one job**
   - Go to **Components**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - There is no role check, although normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the office, the vessel’s office work-order generation switch must be ON. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.

3. **Unplanned work-order creation**
   - Go to **Work Orders**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete **Part A** and click **Save** to store it as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to create it.
   - There is no role check or switch requirement; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The published June office manual describes the on-demand and unplanned procedures but does not state the office role or switch conditions. The planned-generation procedure and those conditions are from the draft code-derived guidance, based on application revision **cf5241ad6**.

**Source:**  
- Planned scheduled generation — draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, items 1 and prerequisites.  
- One-job on-demand generation — draft code-derived guidance, same section, item 2; June Office Sail Admin Manual, *1.1.5 Work Orders* material as referenced by the guidance.  
- Unplanned creation — June Office Sail Admin Manual, *1.1.5.2 How to Create an Unplanned Work Order*, p. 29; draft code-derived guidance, same section, item 3.

Screenshot of the Work Order Form - Unplanned showing the Save button highlighted with "Click the Save button to save it as a draft" and the Submit Work Order button highlighted with "After filling the form, click here to create an unplanned work order."

Figure 44

Page 29 of 64

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.32))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 49)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 49)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 49)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 49)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with four callout boxes: 1. Click on the 'Work Order'. 2. Click here to select a vessel. 3. Choose the required field from the status tab that needs to be completed. 4. The work order form can be opened by clicking the 'Edit' icon or selecting the table row.

Figure 49

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 50)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 50)

Screenshot of the Part B - Work Completion Record form with three callout boxes: 1. Click here to open the 'Part B' section and enter the required details. 2. From here, the user can upload the docs. 3. Use this section to add or select quick inputs and smart suggestions.

Figure 50

\* To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support three ways a work order can come into existence:

1. **Planned or scheduled generation**
   - **On the ship:** the system creates work orders automatically once a day, using each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action is required.
   - **In the office:** nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON.
   - A job cannot receive another work order while it already has an active one.

2. **On-demand generation for one job**
   - Go to **Components**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - There is no specific role check, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the office, the vessel-generation switch must be ON. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.

3. **Unplanned work order**
   - Go to **Work Orders**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store it as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to create it.
   - There is no specific role check and no generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The three-way list and the planned-generation and office-condition details come from **draft, code-derived guidance**. The published June manual excerpt provided here documents the unplanned procedure; the guidance states that the June manuals cover the on-demand and unplanned ways but do not state the office conditions.

**Source:**
- Planned/scheduled generation — *Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order” (draft code-derived guidance, revision cf5241ad).
- One-job on-demand generation — same draft guidance, item 2, with related procedure `office-generate-wo-per-job.md`.
- Unplanned work order — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; also the draft guidance, item 3.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to generate it.
   - No role check or generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The draft code-derived guidance covers all three ways. The June published manuals describe only the on-demand and unplanned procedures and do not state the office conditions above.

**Source:**  
- Planned/scheduled generation — *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, draft code-derived guidance, section “Steps” (repository revision cf5241ad6).  
- On-demand generation — same draft guidance, “Steps”; related procedure `office-generate-wo-per-job.md`.  
- Unplanned work order — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; and the draft guidance, “Steps.”

QUESTION: From the Components screen, how do I raise a work order for one job on a component?

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18))
* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

Screenshot of the Components interface showing component details and a list of jobs. A callout box labeled "1" points to the jobs section, and a callout box labeled "2" points to the 'Generate WO' button.

<u>Figure 22</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Generate WO' button. (Ref Figure 23)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

Screenshot of the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window over the Components interface. A callout box points to the pop-up, indicating where to choose a reason for the Work Order.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 39)

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.32))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 49)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 49)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 49)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 49)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with four callout boxes: 1. Click on the 'Work Order'. 2. Click here to select a vessel. 3. Choose the required field from the status tab that needs to be completed. 4. The work order form can be opened by clicking the 'Edit' icon or selecting the table row.

Figure 49

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 50)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 50)

Screenshot of the Part B - Work Completion Record form with three callout boxes: 1. Click here to open the 'Part B' section and enter the required details. 2. From here, the user can upload the docs. 3. Use this section to add or select quick inputs and smart suggestions.

Figure 50

\* To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

FULL ANSWER:
Use **Generate WO** for the selected job:

1. Open **Components**. On Office, select the vessel first.
2. Open the component in the component tree.
3. In **Part C – Jobs**, find the job.
4. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s row.
5. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, select a reason:
   - Planning – Scheduled ahead of time
   - Breakdown – Emergency repair needed
   - Other – Custom reason
6. The work order is generated and appears on the **Work Orders** screen.

The job must be active and must not already have an active work order. On the **Office** instance, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must also be enabled. On **Ship**, no switch is required. The draft code-derived guidance states that this path has no role check beyond normal sign-in, vessel access, and access to Components; the published manuals do not specify the role requirement. The published manuals also describe the action without stating the Office switch or active-job condition.

**Source:**  
- Draft code-derived guidance, **“Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page**.  
- **PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026**, p.18.  
- **PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026**, p.16.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18))
* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

Screenshot of the Components interface showing component details and a list of jobs. A callout box labeled "1" points to the jobs section, and a callout box labeled "2" points to the 'Generate WO' button.

<u>Figure 22</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Generate WO' button. (Ref Figure 23)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

Screenshot of the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window over the Components interface. A callout box points to the pop-up, indicating where to choose a reason for the Work Order.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

---

* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 18)
* The user can click the ‘Generate WO’ button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 18)

screenshot: Software interface showing component details and a list of jobs with callouts. Callout 1: "1. Here, the user can view all associated jobs and their details." Callout 2: "2. Click here to initiate a job for the chosen component."

<u>Figure 18</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the ‘Generate WO’ button. (Ref Figure 19)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 19), and the Work Order will be generated.

screenshot: Software interface showing a 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window with reason options. Callout: "From here, the user can choose a reason to create the Work Order."

<u>Figure 19</u>

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.27))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 39)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 39)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 39)

screenshot: Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard showing status tabs and work order list with instructional callouts

Figure 39

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 40)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 40)

screenshot: Part B - Work Completion Record form showing risk assessment and work duration fields with instructional callouts

Figure 40

☞ To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
To raise a work order for one job from **Components**:

1. Ensure the job is **active** and has **no active work order**.  
   - **Office:** the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.  
   - **Ship:** no switch is required.  
   - The draft code-derived guidance reports no additional role check; this differs from the Office manual context, which is for Sail Admin users.
2. Open **Components**. On Office, select the vessel first.
3. Open the component in the component tree.
4. In **Part C – Jobs**, find the required job.
5. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s Actions column.
6. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, select a reason: **Planning - Scheduled ahead of time**, **Breakdown - Emergency repair needed**, or **Other - Custom reason**.
7. The work order is generated and appears on the **Work Orders** screen.

The system refuses duplicate work orders; it also refuses an inactive job. The June manuals describe the button without stating the Office switch or active-job condition; those conditions come from the draft code-derived guidance.

On a **Ship** instance, the system can also create planned work orders automatically through its daily job-due scan; this does not run on Office.

**Source:**  
- One-job manual generation: draft code-derived guidance, *“Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page*; Office manual, p. 18; Vessel Specific manual, p. 16.  
- Automatic ship generation: draft code-derived guidance, *Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan*.

FULL ANSWER:
From **Components**, use **Generate WO** for the selected job.

1. Open **Components**. On Office, select the vessel first.
2. Open the component in the component tree.
3. In **Part C – Jobs**, find the job. Its task type, frequency, and last/next due dates are shown.
4. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s row.
5. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, choose one reason:
   - Planning – Scheduled ahead of time
   - Breakdown – Emergency repair needed
   - Other – Custom reason
6. The work order is generated and appears under **Work Orders**.

The job must be **active** and must not already have an **active work order**. You must be signed in and have access to the vessel and Components page. On **Office**, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON; on **Ship**, no switch or role check is required. The draft code-derived guidance says this path has no role guard, while the published June manuals do not state these switch and role conditions.

Other supported creation paths are the ship’s automatic daily job-due scan and Office’s whole-vessel **Generate Now** function.

**Source:**  
- Per-job **Generate WO** from Components: draft code-derived guidance, *Generate WO for one job — from the Components page*; Office PMS User Manual, p. 18; Vessel-Specific PMS User Manual, p. 16.  
- Automatic ship generation: draft code-derived guidance, *Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan*.

- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
To raise a work order for one job from **Components**:

1. Ensure the job is **active** and has **no active work order**. You must also be signed in and have access to the vessel and Components page.
   - **Office:** select the vessel first, and the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.
   - **Ship:** no switch or role check is required.
2. Open **Components** and select the component from the component tree.
3. In **Part C – Jobs**, find the required job.
4. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s **Actions** column.
5. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, select a reason:
   - Planning – Scheduled ahead of time
   - Breakdown – Emergency repair needed
   - Other – Custom reason
6. The work order is generated and appears on the **Work Orders** screen.

The draft code-derived guidance says this path has no Sail Admin role restriction. The June manuals describe the action without stating the Office switch requirement; the switch requirement comes from the confirmed application code guidance. Other supported generation paths are the ship’s automatic daily due-date scan and Office whole-vessel **Generate Now**.

**Source:**  
- Per-job **Generate WO**: draft code-derived guidance, *Generate WO for one job — from the Components page*; Office manual, p.18; Vessel manual, p.16.  
- Automatic ship generation: draft code-derived guidance, *Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan*.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
To raise a work order for one job from **Components**:

1. Ensure the job is **active** and has **no active work order**.
   - **Office:** the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.
   - **Ship:** no switch is required.
   - The draft code-derived guidance states that no specific role check applies; normal sign-in, vessel access, and Components-page access are still required.
2. Open **Components**. On Office, select the vessel first.
3. Open the component in the component tree.
4. In **Part C – Jobs**, find the required job.
5. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s Actions column.
6. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, select a reason:
   - Planning – Scheduled ahead of time
   - Breakdown – Emergency repair needed
   - Other – Custom reason
7. The work order is generated and appears on the **Work Orders** screen.

If the job is inactive, generation is refused. A duplicate is also refused when the job already has an active work order. The June published manuals do not state the Office switch requirement; the draft code-derived guidance says it is required on Office.

Other supported routes are the ship’s automatic daily job-due scan and Office whole-vessel **Generate Now**; those are not the per-job Components procedure.

**Source:**  
- Draft code-derived guidance, *“Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page*, sections “Prerequisites,” “Steps,” and “Exceptions / edge cases.”  
- *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, p. 18.  
- *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, p. 16.

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 40)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 40)

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
From **Components**, raise a work order for one job as follows:

1. Open **Components**. On **Office**, select the vessel first, then open the component in the component tree.
2. In **Part C – Jobs**, locate the job. Its task type, frequency, and last/next due dates are shown.
3. Click **Generate WO** in that job’s row.
4. In the **Generate Work Order** pop-up, select a reason:
   - **Planning – Scheduled ahead of time**
   - **Breakdown – Emergency repair needed**
   - **Other – Custom reason**
5. The work order is generated and appears on the **Work Orders** screen.

The selected job must not already have an active work order. The draft code-derived guidance also states that the job must be active. On **Office**, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be enabled; on **Ship**, no switch or role check is required. The published June manuals describe the action as unconditional and do not state these additional prerequisites; this is a documented difference between the manual and the draft code-derived guidance. No Sail Admin role is required on this **Generate WO** path according to the draft guidance.

Other supported planned-work-order routes are the ship’s automatic daily job-due scan and office-side generation for a whole vessel using **Generate Now**.

**Source:**  
- “Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page, draft code-derived guidance, **Prerequisites and Steps**.  
- *Technical User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, **p.18**.  
- *Technical User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, **p.16**.
